.

Fix a tubular neighbourhood
(\nu Y,\varphi)
of
Y
. By taking
\nu Y
sufficiently small, we may assume that it intersects each of the discs
\Delta_{1},\ldots,\Delta_{m}
, and
\Delta_{L}
only in a collar of their boundary. Then the identification
\varphi\colon DN_{X}Y\to\nu Y
specifies a section of
\mathchoice{{f\rule[-4.9583pt]{0.0pt}{13.19443pt}\mkern 2.0mu\vrule width=0.60277pt\mkern 2.5mu}{\rule[-4.09721pt]{0.0pt}{12.33334pt}}^{*}_{C_{i}}}{{f\rule[-4.9583pt]{0.0pt}{13.19443pt}\mkern 2.0mu\vrule width=0.60277pt\mkern 2.5mu}{\rule[-4.09721pt]{0.0pt}{12.33334pt}}^{*}_{C_{i}}}{{f\rule[-4.37495pt]{0.0pt}{10.52774pt}\mkern 2.0mu\vrule width=0.60277pt\mkern 2.5mu}{\rule[-3.51387pt]{0.0pt}{9.66666pt}}^{*}_{C_{i}}}{{f\rule[-3.98607pt]{0.0pt}{8.74997pt}\mkern 2.0mu\vrule width=0.60277pt\mkern 2.5mu}{\rule[-3.12498pt]{0.0pt}{7.88889pt}}^{*}_{C_{i}}}SN_{X}Y
for each for
i=1,\ldots,n
given by the normal direction into
\Delta_{i}
, as well as a section of
\mathchoice{{f\rule[-4.9583pt]{0.0pt}{13.19443pt}\mkern 2.0mu\vrule width=0.60277pt\mkern 2.5mu}{\rule[-4.09721pt]{0.0pt}{12.33334pt}}^{*}_{Q_{j}}}{{f\rule[-4.9583pt]{0.0pt}{13.19443pt}\mkern 2.0mu\vrule width=0.60277pt\mkern 2.5mu}{\rule[-4.09721pt]{0.0pt}{12.33334pt}}^{*}_{Q_{j}}}{{f\rule[-4.37495pt]{0.0pt}{10.52774pt}\mkern 2.0mu\vrule width=0.60277pt\mkern 2.5mu}{\rule[-3.51387pt]{0.0pt}{9.66666pt}}^{*}_{Q_{j}}}{{f\rule[-3.98607pt]{0.0pt}{8.74997pt}\mkern 2.0mu\vrule width=0.60277pt\mkern 2.5mu}{\rule[-3.12498pt]{0.0pt}{7.88889pt}}^{*}_{Q_{j}}}SN_{X}Y
for
j=1,2
given by the direction into
\Delta_{L}
. These combine to give a section of
\mathchoice{{f\rule[-4.9583pt]{0.0pt}{13.19443pt}\mkern 2.0mu\vrule width=0.60277pt\mkern 2.5mu}{\rule[-4.09721pt]{0.0pt}{12.33334pt}}^{*}_{\partial\widehat{\Sigma}\setminus C}}{{f\rule[-4.9583pt]{0.0pt}{13.19443pt}\mkern 2.0mu\vrule width=0.60277pt\mkern 2.5mu}{\rule[-4.09721pt]{0.0pt}{12.33334pt}}^{*}_{\partial\widehat{\Sigma}\setminus C}}{{f\rule[-4.37495pt]{0.0pt}{10.52774pt}\mkern 2.0mu\vrule width=0.60277pt\mkern 2.5mu}{\rule[-3.51387pt]{0.0pt}{9.66666pt}}^{*}_{\partial\widehat{\Sigma}\setminus C}}{{f\rule[-3.98607pt]{0.0pt}{8.74997pt}\mkern 2.0mu\vrule width=0.60277pt\mkern 2.5mu}{\rule[-3.12498pt]{0.0pt}{7.88889pt}}^{*}_{\partial\widehat{\Sigma}\setminus C}}SN_{X}Y
, which can be extended to a section

s\colon\widehat{\Sigma}\to\mathchoice{{f\rule[-4.9583pt]{0.0pt}{13.19443pt}\mkern 2.0mu\vrule width=0.60277pt\mkern 2.5mu}{\rule[-4.09721pt]{0.0pt}{12.33334pt}}^{*}_{\widehat{\Sigma}}}{{f\rule[-4.9583pt]{0.0pt}{13.19443pt}\mkern 2.0mu\vrule width=0.60277pt\mkern 2.5mu}{\rule[-4.09721pt]{0.0pt}{12.33334pt}}^{*}_{\widehat{\Sigma}}}{{f\rule[-4.37495pt]{0.0pt}{10.52774pt}\mkern 2.0mu\vrule width=0.60277pt\mkern 2.5mu}{\rule[-3.51387pt]{0.0pt}{9.66666pt}}^{*}_{\widehat{\Sigma}}}{{f\rule[-3.98607pt]{0.0pt}{8.74997pt}\mkern 2.0mu\vrule width=0.60277pt\mkern 2.5mu}{\rule[-3.12498pt]{0.0pt}{7.88889pt}}^{*}_{\widehat{\Sigma}}}SN_{X}Y,

since it has not been prescribed on the boundary component
C
. So
\mathchoice{{f\rule[-4.9583pt]{0.0pt}{13.19443pt}\mkern 2.0mu\vrule width=0.60277pt\mkern 2.5mu}{\rule[-4.09721pt]{0.0pt}{12.33334pt}}_{\widehat{\Sigma}}}{{f\rule[-4.9583pt]{0.0pt}{13.19443pt}\mkern 2.0mu\vrule width=0.60277pt\mkern 2.5mu}{\rule[-4.09721pt]{0.0pt}{12.33334pt}}_{\widehat{\Sigma}}}{{f\rule[-4.37495pt]{0.0pt}{10.52774pt}\mkern 2.0mu\vrule width=0.60277pt\mkern 2.5mu}{\rule[-3.51387pt]{0.0pt}{9.66666pt}}_{\widehat{\Sigma}}}{{f\rule[-3.98607pt]{0.0pt}{8.74997pt}\mkern 2.0mu\vrule width=0.60277pt\mkern 2.5mu}{\rule[-3.12498pt]{0.0pt}{7.88889pt}}_{\widehat{\Sigma}}}
can be pushed off
Y
in the direction of
s
. But by construction, this push-off is into the discs
\Delta_{1},\ldots,\Delta_{m}
, and
\Delta_{L}
which only meet
Y
transversely in their boundaries, so it extends to a push-off of
f\colon\Sigma\to X
off
Y
.
For ease of reference, we call this push-off
f^{s}_{+}\colon\Sigma\to\overline{X\setminus\nu Y}
.

We now construct the embedding
h\colon D^{2}\to X
. Let

K^{s}_{+}\coloneq f^{s}_{+}(\partial\Sigma)\subset S\nu Y

be the embedded circle given by pushing
K
off
Y
in the direction of
s
. Since
K^{s}_{+}
is bounded by
f^{s}_{+}(\Sigma)
, the circle
K^{s}_{+}
is null-homologous in
X\setminus Y
. But since
\pi_{1}(X\setminus Y)
is cyclic and hence abelian,
K^{s}_{+}
is in fact null-homotopic in
X\setminus Y
. Thus we can find a null-homotopy of
K^{s}_{+}
in
X\setminus Y
, given by a map
D^{2}\to X\setminus Y
which restricts to
K^{s}_{+}
on the boundary. We may extend this null-homotopy linearly through
\nu Y
so that it restricts on the boundary
K\subset Y
, and perturb it to give a smooth embedding
h\colon D^{2}\to X
. Then
h(D^{2})
spans
K
into
X\setminus Y
as required.

The final thing that remains to check is that the push-off
f^{s}_{+}\colon\Sigma\to X
extends to a push-off of
f\cup h\colon\Sigma\cup D^{2}\to X
. But this is true by the construction of
h
, since
f^{s}_{+}
pushes
\partial\Sigma
off in the inwards direction of
h(D^{2})
.

We have therefore constructed both the embedding
h\colon D^{2}\to X
and the map
f\colon\Sigma\to X
satisfying the conditions required.

The proof the concludes as outlined at the start. Since
\Sigma\cup h(D^{2})
is a closed orientable surface and the map
f\cup h
can be homotoped into
X\setminus Y
,
??
and the assumption that
\langle w_{2}(X\setminus Y),\sigma\rangle=0
for all
\sigma\in H_{2}(X\setminus Y;\mathbb{Z})
implies that
(f\cup h)^{*}TX
is trivial. Since we have shown that the framing of
\mathchoice{{f\rule[-4.9583pt]{0.0pt}{13.19443pt}\mkern 2.0mu\vrule width=0.60277pt\mkern 2.5mu}{\rule[-4.09721pt]{0.0pt}{12.33334pt}}^{*}_{\partial\Sigma}}{{f\rule[-4.9583pt]{0.0pt}{13.19443pt}\mkern 2.0mu\vrule width=0.60277pt\mkern 2.5mu}{\rule[-4.09721pt]{0.0pt}{12.33334pt}}^{*}_{\partial\Sigma}}{{f\rule[-4.37495pt]{0.0pt}{10.52774pt}\mkern 2.0mu\vrule width=0.60277pt\mkern 2.5mu}{\rule[-3.51387pt]{0.0pt}{9.66666pt}}^{*}_{\partial\Sigma}}{{f\rule[-3.98607pt]{0.0pt}{8.74997pt}\mkern 2.0mu\vrule width=0.60277pt\mkern 2.5mu}{\rule[-3.12498pt]{0.0pt}{7.88889pt}}^{*}_{\partial\Sigma}}
determined by the
\Pin^{-}
-structure
\mathfrak{p}
on
Y
extends over
\Sigma
, this implies that the framing of
\mathchoice{{h\rule[-3.01385pt]{0.0pt}{11.24998pt}\mkern 2.0mu\vrule width=0.60277pt\mkern 2.5mu}{\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{10.3889pt}}^{*}_{S^{1}}}{{h\rule[-3.01385pt]{0.0pt}{11.24998pt}\mkern 2.0mu\vrule width=0.60277pt\mkern 2.5mu}{\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{10.3889pt}}^{*}_{S^{1}}}{{h\rule[-3.01385pt]{0.0pt}{9.16664pt}\mkern 2.0mu\vrule width=0.60277pt\mkern 2.5mu}{\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{8.30556pt}}^{*}_{S^{1}}}{{h\rule[-3.01385pt]{0.0pt}{7.77776pt}\mkern 2.0mu\vrule width=0.60277pt\mkern 2.5mu}{\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{6.91667pt}}^{*}_{S^{1}}}TX
extends over
D^{2}
. That is, the framing of
\mathchoice{{TX\rule[-3.01385pt]{0.0pt}{11.13885pt}\mkern 2.0mu\vrule width=0.60277pt\mkern 2.5mu}{\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{10.27777pt}}_{K}}{{TX\rule[-3.01385pt]{0.0pt}{11.13885pt}\mkern 2.0mu\vrule width=0.60277pt\mkern 2.5mu}{\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{10.27777pt}}_{K}}{{TX\rule[-3.01385pt]{0.0pt}{9.08888pt}\mkern 2.0mu\vrule width=0.60277pt\mkern 2.5mu}{\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{8.2278pt}}_{K}}{{TX\rule[-3.01385pt]{0.0pt}{7.72221pt}\mkern 2.0mu\vrule width=0.60277pt\mkern 2.5mu}{\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{6.86113pt}}_{K}}
determined by
\mathfrak{p}
extends over the disc
h(D^{2})
which spans
K
into
X\setminus Y
. Therefore any abstract
\Pin^{-}
-surgery on
K
can be realised over
h(D^{2})
.

Since
K
was arbitrary, we have shown that any abstract 1-surgery which is a
\Pin^{-}
-surgery with respect to
\mathfrak{p}
can be realised ambiently in
X
.
∎

Remark
\thetheorem
.

The statement of Proposition 5.1 of
[
30
]
omits the assumption that
\pi_{1}(X\setminus Y)
is cyclic and generated by a meridian to
Y
, or any possible weaker assumption. Without such an assumption, one must check that the chosen stable framing of
N_{X}Y
over each embedded circle extends over any map from any compact surface, not just orientable surfaces. This is the claim made in point (3) of the proof, but in fact the proof only verifies it for orientable surfaces. This has no effect on the rest of the proof of Theorem 6.1 of
[
30
]
, since Proposition 5.1 is only applied in cases where the missing assumption is satisfied.

Remark
\thetheorem
.

There is also an error in point (1) of the proof of Proposition 5.1 of
[
30
]
. The proof claims that, for any two discs
\Delta_{1}
and
\Delta_{2}
spanning some embedded circle
K\subset Y
into
X\setminus Y
, the 2-sphere
\Delta_{1}\cup\Delta_{2}
can be pushed off
Y
. This is used to argue, similarly to the proof of
??
above, that the unique framings of some subbundles of
TX
on
\Delta_{1}
and
\Delta_{2}
restrict to the same framing on
K
. This is true whenever
H_{2}(X;\mathbb{Z})=0
by an argument involving Alexander duality, and in particular is true in Kervaire’s case of
X=D^{5}
. In general however, it is not always possible to push
\Delta_{1}\cup\Delta_{2}
off
Y
. This is fixed by arguing in the proof of
??
above, carefully constructing a disc
\Delta
spanning each embedded curve
K
into
X\setminus Y
.

We give an explicit counterexample. Let
X=S^{2}\times S^{2}\times I
and
Y=S^{2}\times\{\pt\}\times I
. Then the complement
X\setminus Y\cong S^{2}\times\mathring{D}^{2}\times I
, and so
\pi_{1}(X\setminus Y)=\{1\}
and
w_{2}(X\setminus Y)=0
. Let
K\subset Y
be any embedded circle, and let
s,s^{\prime}\colon K\to\mathchoice{{SN_{X}Y\rule[-4.51385pt]{0.0pt}{12.63885pt}\mkern 2.0mu\vrule width=0.60277pt\mkern 2.5mu}{\rule[-3.65277pt]{0.0pt}{11.77777pt}}_{K}}{{SN_{X}Y\rule[-4.51385pt]{0.0pt}{12.63885pt}\mkern 2.0mu\vrule width=0.60277pt\mkern 2.5mu}{\rule[-3.65277pt]{0.0pt}{11.77777pt}}_{K}}{{SN_{X}Y\rule[-4.01941pt]{0.0pt}{10.09444pt}\mkern 2.0mu\vrule width=0.60277pt\mkern 2.5mu}{\rule[-3.15833pt]{0.0pt}{9.23335pt}}_{K}}{{SN_{X}Y\rule[-4.7083pt]{0.0pt}{9.41666pt}\mkern 2.0mu\vrule width=0.60277pt\mkern 2.5mu}{\rule[-3.84721pt]{0.0pt}{8.55557pt}}_{K}}
be two sections of the circle normal bundle. After choosing a tubular neighbourhood
(\nu Y,\varphi)
of
Y
, let
K_{+}^{s}\coloneq\varphi s(K)
and
K_{+}^{s^{\prime}}\coloneq\varphi s^{\prime}(K)
be the push-offs of
K
in the directions of
s
and
s^{\prime}
, respectively. Since
X\setminus Y
is simply-connected, we can find two embedded discs in
X\setminus Y
with boundaries
K_{+}^{s}
and
K_{+}^{s^{\prime}}
respectively. By extending these discs by an annulus through
\nu Y
to have boundary
K
, we find two 2-discs
\Delta,\Delta^{\prime}
spanning
K
into
X\setminus Y
.

The algebraic intersection number
[Y]\cdot[\Delta\cup\Delta^{\prime}]\in\mathbb{Z}
is exactly the difference in the framings
s
and
s^{\prime}
. To see this, let
\Sigma\subset X
be the embedded sphere given by perturbing
\Delta\cup\Delta^{\prime}
in the direction of
s
near
K
. Then
\Sigma
intersects
Y
only in transverse isolated points, and the signed count of the intersection points agrees with the number of times
s^{\prime}
twists positively around
s
.

In particular, if
s
and
s^{\prime}
are not isotopic, then
\Delta\cup\Delta^{\prime}
cannot be pushed off of
Y
.

Combining
??
and
??
shows that we can always use
\Pin^{-}
-structures to control ambient surgeries.

Corollary
\thetheorem
.

Suppose that
X
is orientable, and that
\pi_{1}(X\setminus Y)
is cyclic and generated by a meridian to
Y
.
Then
Y
admits a
\Pin^{-}
-structure such that any abstract 1-surgery which is a
\Pin^{-}
-surgery can be performed ambiently.

Proof.

If
w_{2}(X\setminus Y)
acts trivially on
H_{2}(X\setminus Y;\mathbb{Z})
, this is
??
. If it acts non-trivially, all abstract 1-surgeries can be realised ambiently, so we may choose any
\Pin^{-}
-structure on
Y
.
∎

9.
Concordance of surfaces in simply-connected 4-manifolds

In this final section, we prove Theorem
C
as an application of
??
.

Theorem

(Theorem
C
)
.

Let
X
be a simply-connected 4-manifold and let
\Sigma_{0},\Sigma_{1}\subset X
be properly embedded compact connected surfaces. Let
Z\subset\partial X\times I
be a concordance from
\partial\Sigma_{0}
to
\partial\Sigma_{1}
. Then
Z
extends to a concordance from
\Sigma_{0}
to
\Sigma_{1}
if and only if
\Sigma_{0}\cong\Sigma_{1}
and either:

(i)

\Sigma_{0}
and
\Sigma_{1}
are orientable, and
Z
extends to an orientable cobordism from
\Sigma_{0}
to
\Sigma_{1}
; or

(ii)

\Sigma_{0}
and
\Sigma_{1}
are non-orientable, and
Z
extends to a cobordism from
\Sigma_{0}
to
\Sigma_{1}
.

Proof.

The forwards direction is immediate, since a concordance is a special type of cobordism. So we prove the reverse. Let
Y\subset X\times I
be a cobordism from
\Sigma_{0}
to
\Sigma_{1}
extending
Z
, orientable if
\Sigma_{0}
and
\Sigma_{1}
are. By tubing together components, we may assume that
Y
is connected. We will show that if
X
is simply-connected, then there is a sequence of surgeries taking
Y
to
\Sigma_{0}\times I
which can be realised ambiently, giving a concordance from
\Sigma_{0}
to
\Sigma_{1}
.

By
??
, we can perform ambient 0-surgery on
Y
to assume that
\pi_{1}(X\times I\setminus Y)
is cyclic and generated by a meridian to
Y
without affecting the orientability of
Y
. Then by
??
, we can give
Y
a
\Pin^{-}
-structure such that all abstract 1-surgeries which are
\Pin^{-}
-surgeries can be realised ambiently.

In order to apply
??
to find a sequence of 1-surgeries taking
Y
to
\Sigma_{0}\times I
, we must show that there is a
\Pin^{-}
-structure on
\Sigma_{0}\times I
such that
\partial Y
and
\partial(\Sigma_{0}\times I)
are
\Pin^{-}
-diffeomorphic. We consider two cases.

•

If
\Sigma_{0}
and
\Sigma_{1}
are closed, give
\Sigma_{0}\times\{0\}
the
\Pin^{-}
-structure restricted from the one on
\partial Y
. Extend this to a
\Pin^{-}
-structure on
\Sigma_{0}\times I
by
??
(iii). Then both
\Sigma_{1}\times\{1\}\subset\partial Y
and
\Sigma_{0}\times\{1\}\subset\Sigma_{0}\times I
are
\Pin^{-}
-cobordant to
\Sigma_{0}\times\{0\}
, so they are
\Pin^{-}
-diffeomorphic by
??
. In particular,
\partial Y
and
\partial(\Sigma_{0}\times I)
are
\Pin^{-}
-diffeomorphic.

•

If
\Sigma_{0}
and
\Sigma_{1}
have non-empty boundary, then
\partial Y
is connected. Then for any
\Pin^{-}
-structure on
\Sigma_{0}\times I
, the
\Pin^{-}
-structures on
\partial Y
and
\partial(\Sigma_{0}\times I)
are both null-cobordant. So again,
??
says that
\partial Y
and
\partial(\Sigma_{0}\times I)
are
\Pin^{-}
-diffeomorphic.

Thus
??
gives a sequence of
\Pin^{-}
-surgeries taking
Y
to
\Sigma_{0}\times I
. Each these are 1-surgeries, and we can perform each of these surgeries ambiently in
X\times I
. In fact, we can perform them all simultaneously, since 2-discs are generically disjoint in
X\times I\setminus Y
and 1-surgery does not change the fundamental group by
??
.

Performing these surgeries ambiently in
X\times I
yields a properly embedded 3-manifold in
X\times I
with boundary
\partial Y
, diffeomorphic to
\Sigma_{0}\times I
. This is the required concordance from
\Sigma_{0}
to
\Sigma_{1}
.
∎
